does not occur in
\nu
for
i\geq 2n+1
, we have for
1\leq j\leq 2n
that

\lambda_{4n+1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{4n+2}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2n+2-j)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2n+2-j)}=\mu_{2j}^{(2n+2-j)}=0
.
In particular, for
0\leq j\leq n-1
,

\displaystyle p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+2}^{(2n+1-2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)

\displaystyle=p_{1}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+4}^{(2n-2j)}\right]^{(k,1)}\right)\,,

\displaystyle p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+4}^{(2n-2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\lambda_{4j+5}^{(2n-1-2j)}\right]^{(k,1)}

By (
5.2.2
),

p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+2}^{(2n+1-2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)=p_{1}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+4}^{(2n-2j)}\right]^{(k,1)}\right)\Longrightarrow p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+2}^{(2n-2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)\succeq\left[\lambda_{4j+3}^{(2n-1-2j)}\right]^{(k,1)}\,\cdot

By iterating (
5.2.6
) plus (
5.2.2
), we obtain by induction on
i
for
n\geq i>j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+2}^{(2i-2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)

\displaystyle\succeq\left[\lambda_{4j+3}^{(2i-1-2j)}\right]^{(k,1)}\,,

\displaystyle p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{4j+4}^{(2i-2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)

\displaystyle\succeq\left[\lambda_{4j+5}^{(2i-1-2j)}\right]^{(k,1)}\,\cdot

In particular, the result follows from the case
i=j+1
. Note that
\lambda_{4n}^{(1)}=0
, and by the choice of
t
, we have
t
is at most equal to
2n
.

5.2.2.
Existence and the uniqueness of the reverse image

Let
\lambda
be a sequence in
\mathcal{L}^{(k,1)}
with
2t
parts, and suppose that the first part
\lambda_{1}<a^{(k,1)}_{2n+2}=a_{n+1}^{(k-2)}
.
Since by (
4.4.14
) and (
4.4.6
),

we necessarily have by Lemma

.
Let us set

5.3.2.
Existence and the uniqueness of the reverse image

Let

we necessarily have by Lemma
4.15
that
\left[\lambda_{2j}\right]^{(k,1)}\prec(\delta_{i,n+2-j})_{i\geq 1}
.
Let us set
\lambda_{j}^{(1)}=\lambda_{j}

.
By considering the pair

and

for

. Suppose now that, for some

(1)

and
m_{1}^{(j)}
from fact
(2)
and we have

(2)

Now assume that we retrieve the datum
\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2i-2)}
for some

, and that

We consecutively retrieve
\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2i-2)}\right]^{(1,k)}
, then
\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}
from
m_{2i-3}^{(j+1)}
and fact
(1.b)
, then
m_{2i-2}^{(j)}
from fact

, then

\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2i)}\right]^{(1,k)}
from (

). Moreover, by (

) and (

), we have

p_{2}\left(\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)\succeq 0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j+2}^{(2i-2)}\right]^{(k,1)}\text{ and then }p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2i)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)\succeq p_{1}\left(\left[\mu_{2j+1}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}\right)\cdot

\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}
for all

.

6.
Well-definedness of
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{2n}

We first give an interpretation of the insertion of the parts
b^{(k,l)}_{i}
into the pairs
(\lambda_{2j},\lambda_{2j-1})
in terms of the admissible words. Denote by
(\lambda_{2j}^{(i)},\lambda_{2j-1}^{(i)})
the pairs
(\lambda_{2j},\lambda_{2j-1})
after the insertion of all the parts
b^{(k,l)}_{i}
, and let
m_{i}^{(j)}
denote the number of parts
b^{(k,l)}_{i}
inserted into the pair
(\lambda_{2j},\lambda_{2j-1})
. Note that
m_{i}^{(n)}
equals the number of occurrences of
b^{(k,l)}_{i}
in
\nu
, and the image by
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{2n}
consists of
(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2n}
.

6.1.
The case
k,l\geq 2

Proposition 6.1
.

For
n\geq j\geq 1
,

(1)

for

,

, and

(2)

2j\geq i\geq 2
,

\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(i)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\in\,_{i}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}\,\cdot

(6.1.1)

(a)

Furthermore,

\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{i}^{(j)},\,_{i}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j},\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i+1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\right)
. In particular,
\lambda_{2j}^{(2j)}=m_{2j}^{(j)}\cdot a^{(k,l)}_{2n}
.

(b)

Reciprocally,
{}_{i+1}\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{i-1}^{(j-1)},\,_{i+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j},\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i+1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\right)
.

(3)

Finally,
\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}
,

(a)

and
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{1}^{(j)},\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j},\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\right)
.

(b)

Reciprocally,
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{1}^{(j)},\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j},0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\right)
.

Analogously to (
5.1.2
), for
1<j\leq n
and
1<i\leq 2j-1
,

00\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}\preceq\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i+1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\Longleftrightarrow 00\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(i-1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}\preceq\,_{i+1}\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\,\cdot

(6.1.2)

To prove that
(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2n}
belongs to
\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n}
, as by (
6.1.1
) and fact
(3)
,

0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\preceq\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\,,

it suffices to prove that for
n\geq j>1
,

00\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}\preceq\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}
.
This holds by recursively using (
6.1.2
) and fact
(1)
, as

(0)_{i=1}^{2j}=00\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(2j-1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}\preceq\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2j)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}
.

Let us now construct the inverse image. Let
(\lambda_{j})_{j=1}^{2n}
be a sequence in
\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n}
, and set
\lambda_{j}^{(1)}=\lambda_{j}
.
By considering the pair
(\lambda_{1}^{(1)},\lambda_{2}^{(1)})
, we retrieve
m_{1}^{(1)},\lambda_{2}^{(2)}
by fact
(3)
, and since by (
4.4.18
),
0\cdot\left[\lambda_{1}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{1}=\left[\lambda_{2}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2}=(0,m_{2}^{(1)})
, we have
m_{2}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2}^{(2)}/a^{(k,l)}_{2}=\lambda_{1}^{(1)}
. Assume now that, for
2\leq j\leq n
we retrieve
(\lambda_{2j-2}^{i})_{i=1}^{2j-2}
(and then
(m_{i}^{(j-1)})_{i=1}^{2j-2}
by fact
(1.a)
). By fact
(3)
, we retrieve
\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
and
m_{1}^{(j)}
, and since
00\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}\preceq\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}
. Using Lemma
2.7
, fact
(1.b)
and (
6.1.2
), we recursively retrieve
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}
from
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i-1)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}
and
m_{i-2}^{(j-1)}
for
3\leq i\leq 2j
. By fact
(2.a)
, we recover
m_{i}^{(j)}
for
1\leq i\leq 2j-1
. Finally, as
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2j)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\in\,_{2j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}
, we set
m_{2j}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2j)}/a^{(k,l)}_{2j}
. Therefore, recursively on
j
, we recover the datum
(m_{i}^{(n)})_{i=1}^{2n}
and we conclude.

Proposition 6.3
.

For
n\geq j\geq 1
,

(1)

for
n+j+1\geq i>2j\geq 1
,
m_{i}^{(j)}=0
, and
\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(i)}=0
.

(2)

for
2j\geq i\geq 3
, we have

\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}=p_{1}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(i)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)=0\cdot p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)\in\,_{\lceil i-1/2\rceil}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2j}\,\cdot

(6.2.1)

(a)

For

, let
\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2i)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{2i}^{(j)},\,_{+(i-1)}\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}_{2j+1-2i},\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2i+1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)
.

(i)

Then,
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2i)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}=p_{1}\left(\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2i)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)

and

\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}=((\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},m_{2j}^{(j)}),(0)_{u=1}^{j-1})\,\cdot

(ii)

Furthermore,
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{2i-1}^{(j)},\,_{i}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2j},\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2i)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}\right)
.

(b)

Reciprocally, for
j\geq i\geq 2
, set

\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}=\left(p_{1}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right),\,_{i}p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2i-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)\right)\,\cdot

(i)

,

(ii)

and

.

(3)

\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{2}^{(j)},\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}_{2j-1},\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(3)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)
.

(a)

Then,
\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}=p_{1}\left(\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}\right)
.

(b)

\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}

(a)

and,

.

.

.

Example 6.6
.

For

with

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2i-2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j}\right)\succeq p_{1}\left(\left[\mu_{2j-2}^{(2i-3)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-2}\right)\,\cdot

(6.3.4)

To prove that
(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2n}
belongs to
\mathcal{L}^{(1,k)}_{2n}
, as by (
6.3.1
) and fact
(3)
,

p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j}\right)\succeq\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j-1}\,,

it suffices to prove that for
n\geq j>1
,

p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j}\right)\succeq p_{1}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-2}\right)
. This holds by using recursively (
6.3.4
) and (
6.3.2
), since by fact
(1)
,
(0)_{u=1}^{j}=p_{1}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(2j-1)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-2}\right)\succeq p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2j)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j}\right)
.

To construct the inverse map, first build
m_{1}^{(1)}
from fact
(3)
and
m_{2}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2}^{(2)}/a^{(1,k)}_{2}=\lambda_{1}^{(2)}
. Then, recursively retrieve,
\lambda_{2j}^{(2)},m_{1}^{(j)}
from fact
(3)
, then
(\lambda_{2j}^{(i)})_{i=3}^{(2j)}
from
(m_{i}^{(j-1)})_{j=1}^{(2j-2)}
using fact
(2.b)
, subsequently
(m_{i}^{(j)})_{i=2}^{(2j-1)}
by fact
(2.a)
, and finally set
m_{2j}^{(j)}=\lambda_{2j}^{2j}/a^{(1,k)}_{2j}
.
Therefore, we recover the datum
(m_{i}^{(n)})_{i=1}^{(2j)}
and we conclude.

7.
Well-definedness of
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}

We here consider
n\geq 2
, as
\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{(k,l)}_{1}
and
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{1}=Id
.
Let us give an interpretation of the insertion of the parts
b^{(l,k)}_{i}
into the pairs
(\lambda_{2j+1},\lambda_{2j})
in terms of the admissible words. Denote by
(\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i)},\lambda_{2j}^{(i)})
the pairs
(\lambda_{2j+1},\lambda_{2j})
after the insertion of all the parts
b^{(l,k)}_{i}
, and let
m_{i}^{(j)}
denote the number of parts
b^{(l,k)}_{i}
inserted into the pair
(\lambda_{2j+1},\lambda_{2j})
. Note that
m_{i}^{(n-1)}
equals the number of occurrences of
b^{(l,k)}_{i}
in
\nu\in\mathcal{B}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}
, and the image by
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}
consists of
(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2n-1}
.

7.1.
The case
k,l\geq 2

The proof is similar to Section
6.1
by using the following proposition.

Proposition 7.1
.

For
n-1\geq j\geq 0
,

(1)

for
n+j+1\geq i>2j+1\geq 1
,
m_{i}^{(j)}=0
, and
\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}=0
.

(2)

For
2j+1\geq i\geq 2
,

\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}=0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\in\,_{i}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}\,\cdot

(7.1.1)

(a)

Furthermore,

\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{i}^{(j)},\,_{i}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j+1},\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i+1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}\right)
.

(b)

Reciprocally,
{}_{i+1}\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}=\mathcal{F}\left(m_{i-1}^{(j-1)},\,_{i+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j+1},\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(i+1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}\right)
.

(3)

Finally,
\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
,

(a)

and

.

.

8.
Equivalence to the Bousquet-Mélou–Eriksson recursive bijection

8.1.
Equivalence for
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{n}

Define the function
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{n}
from
\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{n}
to
\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}_{n-1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
as follows.

(1)

For
\lambda=(\lambda_{j})_{j=1}^{2n+1}\in\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}
, set

\begin{cases}m=\lambda_{2n+1}-\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}\lambda_{2n}\right\rceil\,,\\
\nu_{2j}=\lambda_{2j}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j\leq n\\
\nu_{2j-1}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2j-1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}\right\rfloor+\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2j-1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}}\lambda_{2j-2}\right\rceil-\lambda_{2j-1}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1<j\leq n\\
\nu_{1}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2}}\lambda_{2}\right\rfloor-\lambda_{1}.\end{cases}

Finally set
\nu=(\nu_{j})_{j=1}^{2n}
and
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
.

(2)

For
\lambda=(\lambda_{j})_{j=1}^{2n}\in\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n}
, set

\begin{cases}m=\lambda_{2n}-\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}}\lambda_{2n-1}\right\rceil\,,\\
\nu_{2j-1}=\lambda_{2j-1}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j\leq n\\
\nu_{2j}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j+1}}\lambda_{2j+1}\right\rfloor+\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j-1}}\lambda_{2j-1}\right\rceil-\lambda_{2j}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j<n\,.\end{cases}

Finally set
\nu=(\nu_{j})_{j=1}^{2n-1}
and
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
.

Lemma 8.1
.

The map
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{n}
describe a bijection from
\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{n}
to
\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}_{n-1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Furthermore, for
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
, we have
\lambda_{e}=\nu_{e}
and
\lambda_{o}=m+k\cdot\nu_{e}-\nu_{o}
,
and for
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
, we have
\lambda_{o}=\nu_{o}
and
\lambda_{e}=m+l\cdot\nu_{o}-\nu_{e}
.

By setting

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{n}}(x,y)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{n}}x^{|\lambda|_{o}}y^{|\lambda|_{e}}\,,

Lemma
8.1
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-y}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}}(xy^{l},y^{-1})\,,

(8.1.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-x}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n}}(x^{-1},yx^{k})\,\cdot

(8.1.2)

In the recursive process, as
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{1}}(x,y)=1/(1-x)
,
a^{(k,l)}_{i}=i
for
i\in\{0,1\}
, and
la^{(l,k)}_{i}-a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}=a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}
for
i\geq 1
, the transformations occuring in the generating functions are

\displaystyle x^{a^{(l,k)}_{i}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}}\mapsto x^{a^{(l,k)}_{i}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b^{(l,k)}_{i}\mapsto b^{(k,l)}_{i+1}

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq 2n-1\,,

\displaystyle y=x^{a^{(l,k)}_{0}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{1}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\text{addition of parts }b^{(k,l)}_{1}

in (
8.1.1
)

\displaystyle x^{a^{(l,k)}_{i-1}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}\mapsto x^{a^{(l,k)}_{i+1}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b^{(k,l)}_{i}\mapsto b^{(l,k)}_{i+1}

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq 2n\,,

\displaystyle x=x^{a^{(l,k)}_{1}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{0}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\text{addition of parts }b^{(l,k)}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\text{in \eqref{eq:fineventoodd}}\,\cdot

Therefore, we recursively obtain Theorem
1.4
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Moreover, the bijections
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{n}
induce unique recursive bijections
\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{2n}:\mathcal{B}^{(k,l)}_{2n}\mapsto\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n}
and
\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}:\mathcal{B}^{(l,k)}_{2n}\mapsto\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}
, such that,
for any sequence
(m_{j})_{j=1}^{2n+1}
of non-negative integers, we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}\left(\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n+1}(b^{(l,k)}_{i})^{m_{i}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{2n}\left(\prod_{i=2}^{2n+1}(b^{(k,l)}_{i-1})^{m_{i}}\right),m_{1}\right)

(8.1.3)

\displaystyle\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n}\left(\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{2n}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}(b^{(k,l)}_{i})^{m_{i}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}\left(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}(b^{(l,k)}_{i-1})^{m_{i}}\right),m_{1}\right)

(8.1.4)

The equivalence is then the following.

Theorem 8.2
.

For
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
\Phi^{(k,l)}_{n}=\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{n}
.

Let
(m_{i})_{i=1}^{2n+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2n+1}
. To prove that, it suffices to show (
8.1.3
) and (
8.1.4
) with

instead of
\Lambda^{(k,l)}_{n}
.

(1)

Set
\lambda=(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2n+1}=\Phi^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}(\prod_{i=1}^{2n+1}(b^{(l,k)}_{i})^{m_{i}})
, and
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
. We here refer to the notations of Section
7
for
\lambda
with
\lambda_{j}^{(i)}
and
m_{i}^{(j)}
(and

for the cases
(k,1)

and

instead of

and
m_{i}^{(j)}
(and
\eta_{2j+1}^{(i)}
instead of

for the cases

and
(1,k)
) in Section

. For the proof of (

), we show that, for

and

,
\nu_{2j}^{(i)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(i+1)}
and

.

(2)

Set
\lambda=(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2n}=\Phi^{(k,l)}_{2n}(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}(b^{(k,l)}_{i})^{m_{i}})
, and
\Psi^{(k,l)}_{2n}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
. We here refer to the notations of Section

for
\lambda
with
\lambda_{j}^{(i)}

8.1.1.
The case
k,l\geq 2

(1)

For
1\leq j\leq n
, we have
\nu_{2j}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
by fact
(3)
of Proposition
7.1
. Also, by (
4.4.3
) and (

),

\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}=0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=\left[\left\lceil\frac{a^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rceil\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}\Longleftrightarrow\quad\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}=\left\lceil\frac{a^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rceil\,\cdot

Hence, using fact
(3.a)
of Proposition
7.1
,

\lambda_{2j+1}^{(1)}-\left\lceil\frac{a^{(l,k)}_{2j+1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}\right\rceil=\lambda_{2j+1}^{(1)}-\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}=m_{1}^{(j)}\,\cdot

Thus,
m=m_{1}^{(n)}=m_{1}
, and since
\lambda_{1}^{(1)}=m_{1}^{(0)}
, we have for all
1\leq j\leq n
,

\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}=\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rfloor-m_{1}^{(j-1)}\,\cdot

By (
4.4.3
), this is equivalent to saying that
0\cdot\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}
is the
(m_{1}^{(j-1)})^{th}
sequence that precedes
{}_{2}\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}
in
{}_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}
. Using (
6.1.1
) and fact
(2.b)
of Proposition
7.1
with

, we then have to
\left[\nu_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}=\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(3)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\in\,_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}
. Therefore, since
\nu_{2j}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
, by fact
(2.a)
of Proposition
7.1
and fact
(3,a)
Proposition
6.1
,
n_{1}^{(j)}=m_{2}^{(j)}
.
Moreover, we have for
j=1

n_{2}^{(1)}=\frac{\nu_{2}^{(2)}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2}}=\frac{\lambda_{2}^{(3)}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2}}=m_{3}^{(1)}\,\cdot

Assuming that for some
n-1\geq j>1
,
n_{i}^{(j-1)}=m_{i+1}^{(j-1)}
and
\left[\nu_{2j-2}^{(i+1)}\right]_{2j-2}^{(k,l)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(i+2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}\in\,_{i+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}
for
1\leq i\leq 2j-2
. Then, using fact
(2)
of Propositions
6.1
and
7.1
, we recursively retrieve that
n_{i}^{(j)}=m_{i+1}^{(j)}
and
\left[\nu_{2j}^{(i+1)}\right]_{2j}^{(k,l)}=\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i+2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\in\,_{i+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}
for
2\leq i\leq 2j-1
. Finally,

n_{2j}^{(j)}=\frac{\nu_{2j}^{(2j)}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}=\frac{\lambda_{2j}^{(2j+1)}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2j}}=m_{2j+1}^{(j)}\,,

and
(0)_{u=1}^{2j}=\left[\nu_{2j}^{(2j+1)}\right]_{2j}^{(k,l)}=\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2j+2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\in\,_{2j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}
, and the heredity on
j
holds.

(2)

By definition and fact

of Propositions
6.1
,
\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}
for

. In particular for

n_{1}^{(0)}=\nu_{1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{1}^{(2)}=m_{2}^{(1)}\,\cdot

Similarly to

, by using (

), (

) and fact

of Propositions

and

,

, and for all

,

\nu_{2j-2}^{(2)}=\nu_{2j-2}^{(1)}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2j-2}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}}\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right\rfloor-m_{1}^{(j-1)}\,\cdot

By (
4.4.3
), (

) and fact

of Proposition

,
\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(3)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\in\,_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j-1}
, and since
\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\in\,_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j-1}
, fact

of Proposition

Proposition

yield
n_{1}^{(j-1)}=m_{2}^{(j)}
.

Assume that for some

,

and

for

. Then, using fact

of Propositions

and

, we recursively retrieve that
n_{i}^{(j-1)}=m_{i+1}^{(j)}
and
\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(i+1)}\right]_{2j-1}^{(l,k)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(i+2)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\in\,_{i+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}
for

. Finally,

n_{2j-1}^{(j-1)}=\frac{\nu_{2j-1}^{(2j-1)}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}}=\frac{\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2j)}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}}=m_{2j}^{(j)}\,,

and
(0)_{u=1}^{2j-1}=\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(2j)}\right]_{2j-1}^{(l,k)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2j+1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\in\,_{2j}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}
, and the heredity on

holds.

8.1.2.
The case
(k,1)

(1)

By (

), and (

),
for

,

\displaystyle p_{1}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j+1}\right)=0\cdot p_{2}\left(\left[\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j+1}\right)

\displaystyle=0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}=\left[\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rceil\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}=\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rceil+\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{a^{(1,k)}_{2j+1}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rceil\,\cdot

Hence, using fact
(3.a)
of Proposition
7.3
,

\lambda_{2j+1}^{(1)}-\left\lceil\frac{a^{(1,k)}_{2j+1}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}\right\rceil=\lambda_{2j+1}^{(1)}-\lambda_{2j+1}^{(2)}=m_{1}^{(j)}\,\cdot

Thus,
m=m_{1}^{(n)}=m_{1}
, and since
\lambda_{1}^{(1)}=m_{1}^{(0)}
, we have for all
1\leq j\leq n
,

\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rfloor-m_{1}^{(j-1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}+\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2j-2}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rfloor-m_{1}^{(j-1)}\,\cdot

By (
4.4.6
), this is equivalent to saying that
0\cdot\left[\eta_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}=0\cdot\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}
is the
(m_{1}^{(j-1)})^{th}
sequence that precedes
\left[\mu_{2j+1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j+1}

{}_{+1}\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}
, and using fact

of Proposition

, that sequence is equal to
\left[\mu_{2j+1}^{(3)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j+1}
. Therefore, by applying
p_{1}^{-}
, (
6.2.1
), fact

of Proposition

and (
7.2.1
), we obtain that
\nu_{2j}^{(2)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(3)}
. Since
\nu_{2j}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
, facts

of Proposition

and

of Proposition

yield
n_{1}^{(j)}=m_{2}^{(j)}
. Moreover, we have for

n_{2}^{(1)}=\frac{\nu_{2}^{(2)}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2}}=\frac{\lambda_{2}^{(3)}}{a^{(k,l)}_{2}}=m_{3}^{(1)}\,\cdot

Assuming that for some
n-1\geq j>1
,
n_{i}^{(j-1)}=m_{i+1}^{(j-1)}
and
\left[\nu_{2j-2}^{(i+1)}\right]_{2j-2}^{(k,1)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-2}^{(i+2)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j-2}\in\,_{\lceil(i+1)/2\rceil}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2j-2}
for

.

. We thus retrieve by facts
(2)
that
n_{2}^{(j)}=m_{3}^{(j)}
. Finally, by definitions in facts
(2.b)
,

, and using facts

and facts

imply that
n_{3}^{(j)}=m_{4}^{(j)}
. Iteration of this process leads to
n_{i}^{(j)}=m_{i+1}^{(j)}
and
\left[\nu_{2j}^{(i+1)}\right]_{2j}^{(k,1)}=\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(i+2)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j}\in\,_{\lceil(i+1)/2\rceil}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2j}
for

. Finally,

n_{2j}^{(j)}=\frac{\nu_{2j}^{(2j)}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}=\frac{\lambda_{2j}^{(2j+1)}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}=m_{2j+1}^{(j)}\,,

and
(0)_{u=1}^{2j}=\left[\nu_{2j}^{(2j+1)}\right]_{2j}^{(k,l)}=\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2j+2)}\right]^{(k,l)}_{2j}\in\,_{2j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2j}
, and the heredity on
j
holds.

(2)

By definition and fact

of Proposition
6.3
,
\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}
for

. In particular for

Moreover, by fact

, for

,

\lambda_{2j}^{(1)}-\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2j}}{a^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}}\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right\rceil=m_{1}^{(j)}\,\cdot

Hence,

, and for all

,

\nu_{2j-2}^{(2)}=\nu_{2j-2}^{(1)}=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2j-2}}{a^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}}\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right\rfloor-m_{1}^{(j-1)}\,\cdot

Therefore,
p_{2}(\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1})=\left[\nu_{2j-2}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,1)}_{2j-2}
is the
(m_{1}^{(j-1)})
sequence that precedes
p_{2}(\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-1})
in

of Proposition

, (

) and (

),
\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(3)}
. Then, as

, by facts

of Propositions
6.3
and
7.3
,
n_{1}^{(j-1)}=m_{2}^{(j)}
.

Assume that for some
n-1\geq j\geq 2
,
n_{i}^{(j-2)}=m_{i+1}^{(j-1)}
and
\left[\nu_{2j-3}^{(i+1)}\right]_{2j-3}^{(1,k)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-3}^{(i+2)}\right]^{(1,k)}_{2j-3}\in\,_{+(\lceil(i-1)/2\rceil)}\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}_{2j-3-2\lceil(i-1)/2\rceil}

, and since
n_{1}^{(j-2)}=m_{2}^{(j-1)}
, by fact

,
\left[\eta_{2j-1}^{(3)}\right]_{2j-1}^{(1,k)}=\left[\mu_{2j-1}^{(4)}\right]_{2j-1}^{(1,k)}\in\,_{+(1)}\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}_{2j-3}
. Hence,
\nu_{2j-1}^{(3)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(4)}
, and we recover by facts
(2.a.ii)
that

.
Moreover, since
n_{2}^{(j-2)}=m_{3}^{(j-1)}
, by using facts
(2.b.ii)
, (

) and (

), we obtain
\nu_{2j-1}^{(4)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(5)}
, and for

, using facts
(2)
, we have
n_{3}^{(j-1)}=m_{4}^{(j)}
. Iterating this process, we have

n_{i}^{(j-1)}=m_{i+1}^{(j)}
and
\nu_{2j-1}^{(i+1)}=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(i+2)}
for
2\leq i\leq 2j-2
. Finally,

n_{2j-1}^{(j-1)}=\frac{\nu_{2j-1}^{(2j-1)}}{a^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}}=\frac{\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2j)}}{a^{(1,k)}_{2j-1}}=m_{2j}^{(j)}\,,

and
(0)_{u=1}^{2j-1}=\left[\nu_{2j-1}^{(2j)}\right]_{2j-1}^{(l,k)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2j+1)}\right]^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}\in\,_{2j}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}
, and the heredity on
j
holds.

8.1.3.
The case
(1,k)

The proofs of
(1)
and
(2)
are respectively similar to the proofs of
(2)
and
(1)
in the case
(k,1)
using Propositions
6.5
and
7.5
.

8.2.
Equivalence for
\Phi^{(k,l)}

Define the function
\Psi^{(k,l)}
from
\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}
to
\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
as follows. For
\lambda=(\lambda_{j})_{j=1}^{2t}\in\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}
, if
t=1
, set
\Psi^{(k,l)}(\lambda)=((0,0),\lambda_{1})
. Otherwise, set

\begin{cases}m=\lambda_{1}-\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2}\right\rfloor-1\,,\\
\nu_{2j-1}=\lambda_{2j}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j\leq t-1\\
\nu_{2j}=\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2j}\right\rceil+\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2j+2}\right\rfloor-\lambda_{2j+1}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j<t-1\\
\nu_{2t-2}=\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2t-2}\right\rceil-1-\lambda_{2t-1}.\end{cases}

If
\nu_{2t-2}=0
, define
\nu=(\nu_{j})_{j=1}^{2t-2}
, otherwise, define
\nu_{2t-1}=\nu_{2t}=0
, and
\nu=(\nu_{j})_{j=1}^{2t}
. Finally set

\Psi^{(k,l)}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
. Observe that in the case where
\nu\neq(0,0)
, it has the same number of parts as
\lambda
if and only if its two last part equals
0
, otherwise
\nu
has two less parts than
\lambda
.

We now defined the inverse map by the following. Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\nu=(\nu_{j})_{j=1}^{2t}
. If
\nu=(0,0)
, set

\lambda=(m,0)
. Otherwise, set
t^{\prime}=t
if
\nu_{2t-1}>0
, and
t^{\prime}=t-1
if
\nu_{2t-1}=0
. Note that
t^{\prime}\geq 1
. Then set

\begin{cases}\lambda_{2j}=\nu_{2j-1}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j\leq t^{\prime}\\
\lambda_{1}=\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\nu_{1}\right\rfloor+1+m\,,\\
\lambda_{2j+1}=\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j-1}\right\rceil+\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j+1}\right\rfloor-\nu_{2j}\ \ \text{for}\ \ 1\leq j<t^{\prime}\\
\lambda_{2t^{\prime}+1}=\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2t^{\prime}-1}\right\rceil-1-\nu_{2t^{\prime}}.\end{cases}

We finally set
\lambda_{2t^{\prime}+2}=0
and
\lambda=(\lambda_{j})_{j=1}^{2t^{\prime}+2}
, and one can check that
\lambda
is the reverse image of
(\nu,m)
by
\Psi^{(k,l)}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

The map
\Psi^{(k,l)}
describes a bijection from
\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}
to
\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and for
\Psi^{(k,l)}(\lambda)=(\nu,m)
, we have
\lambda_{e}=\nu_{o}
and
\lambda_{o}=m+l\cdot\nu_{o}-\nu_{e}
.

By setting

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}}(x,y)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}_{n}}x^{|\lambda|_{o}}y^{|\lambda|_{e}}\,,

Lemma
8.3
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-x}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}}(x^{l}y,x^{-1})\,\cdot

(8.2.1)

In the recursive process, as
a^{(k,l)}_{i}=i
for
i\in\{0,1\}
, and
la^{(l,k)}_{i}-a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}=a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}
for
i\geq 1
, the transformations occurring in the generating functions are

\displaystyle x^{a^{(l,k)}_{i}}y^{a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}}\mapsto x^{a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}}y^{a^{(l,k)}_{i}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b^{(l,k)}_{i}\mapsto b^{(k,l)}_{i+1}

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad 1\leq i\,,

\displaystyle x=x^{a^{(k,l)}_{1}}y^{a^{(l,k)}_{0}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\text{addition of parts }b^{(k,l)}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\text{in \eqref{eq:infkltolk}}\,\cdot

Then, by iterating (
8.2.1
), for
(k,l)
then
(l,k)
, we have for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{1-x^{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}y^{a^{(l,k)}_{i-1}}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}}\left(x^{a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}y^{a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}},x^{-a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}}y^{-a^{(l,k)}_{2n-2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\frac{1}{1-x^{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}y^{a^{(l,k)}_{i-1}}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}}\left(x^{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}y^{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}},x^{-a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}y^{-a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}}\right)

We then obtain Theorem
1.5
when
n
tends to
\infty
.
The analogous result for Theorem
8.2
is the following.

Theorem 8.4
.

For sequence
(m_{j})_{j\geq 1}
of non-negative integers with finitely many positive, we have

\Psi^{(k,l)}\left(\Phi^{(k,l)}\left(\prod_{i\geq 1}(b^{(k,l)}_{i})^{m_{i}}\right)\right)=\left(\Phi^{(l,k)}\left(\prod_{i\geq 2}(b^{(l,k)}_{i-1})^{m_{i}}\right),m_{1}\right)

(8.2.2)

Proof.

Let
(m_{j})_{j\geq 1}
be a sequence of non-negative integers with finitely many positive integers, and set

\lambda=\Phi^{(k,l)}\left(\prod_{i\geq 1}(b^{(k,l)}_{i})^{m_{i}}\right)=(\lambda_{j}^{(1)})_{j=1}^{2t}

as defined in Section
5
. Note that
t=1
if and only if
m_{i}=0
for
i\geq 2
, in which case
\lambda=(m_{1},0)
. In that case, as
\nu=(0,0)
, (
8.2.2
) follows.
Suppose now that
t\geq 2
, i.e there exists
i\geq 2
such that
m_{i}>0
. Let
\Psi^{(k,l)}=(\nu,m)
and write
\nu=(\nu_{j})_{j=1}^{2t}
with
\nu_{1}>0=\nu_{2t-1}=\nu_{2t}
(with eventually
\nu_{2t-2}=0
). We here refer to the notations of Section
5
for
\lambda
with
\lambda_{j}^{(i)}
and
m_{i}^{(j)}
(and
\mu_{j}^{(i)}
for the cases
(k,1)
and
(1,k)
), while using for
\nu
the notations
\nu_{j}^{(i)}
and
n_{i}^{(j)}
instead of
\lambda_{j}^{(i)}
and
m_{i}^{(j)}
(and
\eta_{j}^{(i)}
instead of
\mu_{j}^{(i)}
for the cases
(k,1)
and
(1,k)
). For the proof of (
8.2.2
), we show that, for
1\leq j\leq t
and
1\leq i
,
\nu_{2j-1}^{(i)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(i+1)}
and
n_{i}^{(j)}=m_{i+1}^{(j)}
. This already holds for
j=t
, as
0=n_{i}^{(t)}=m_{i+1}^{(t)}
for
i\geq 1
.
The proof is the same as the proof of (
8.1.4
) for all the cases. We here illustrate the case
k,l\geq 2
.

By fact
(2)
of Proposition
5.1
, we then have, for
1\leq j\leq t
, that
\lambda_{(2j)}^{(2)}=\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}
.
Also, by (
4.4.1
),

\left[\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\rfloor+1\right]^{(k,l)}=0\cdot\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}=\left[\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}\,\cdot

Hence, by fact
(2.a)
of Proposition
5.1
,

\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}-\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\rfloor-1=\lambda_{2j-1}^{(1)}-\lambda_{2j-1}^{(2)}=m_{1}^{(j)}\,\cdot

Thus,
m=m_{1}^{(n)}=m_{1}
, and since
\lambda_{2t-1}^{(1)}=m_{1}^{(t)}
, we have for all
1\leq j\leq t-1
,

\nu_{2j}^{(2)}=\nu_{2j}^{(1)}=\left\lceil s^{(k,l)}_{2}\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right\rceil-1-m_{1}^{(j-1)}\,\cdot

By (
4.4.1
), this is equivalent to saying that
0\cdot\left[\nu_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(k,l)}
is the
(m_{1}^{(j-1)})^{th}
sequence that precedes
{}_{2}\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}
in
{}_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}
. Using (
5.1.1
) and fact
(1.b)
of Proposition
5.1
, we then have to
\left[\nu_{2j}^{(2)}\right]^{(l,k)}=\left[\lambda_{2j}^{(3)}\right]^{(l,k)}\in\,_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}
. Therefore, since
\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
, by fact
(1.a)
and fact
(2.a)
Proposition
5.1
,
n_{1}^{(j)}=m_{2}^{(j)}
.

Assume now that, for some
1\leq j<t
,
\nu_{2j+1}^{(i)}=\lambda_{2j+2}^{(i+1)}
and
n_{i}^{(j+1)}=m_{i+1}^{(j+1)}
for
i\geq 1
.
We already have
\nu_{2j-1}^{(1)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(2)}
,
\nu_{2j-1}^{(2)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(3)}
and
n_{1}^{(j)}=m_{2}^{(j)}
. As
n_{1}^{(j-1)}=m_{2}^{(j-1)}
, we thus have by fact
(1.b)
that
\nu_{2j-1}^{(3)}=\lambda_{2j}^{(4)}
, and by fact
(1.a)
,
n_{2}^{(j)}=m_{3}^{(j)}
. By iterating the process, the heredity on
j
holds. In particular, for
j=1
, we retrieve (
8.2.2
).
∎

Appendix A
Proof of technical lemmas

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
2.5

Let
\frac{1}{2}\geq x<1

(1)

For
a\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle 0<(1-x)a-xb\leq 1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\frac{xb}{1-x}<a\leq 1+\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow 1+\left\lfloor\frac{xb}{1-x}\right\rfloor\leq a\leq 1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor\,,

and (
2.2.1
) holds. The proof of (
2.2.2
) comes from the equivalence

x<(1-x)a-xb\leq 1\Longleftrightarrow 0<a-\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\leq 1

Now suppose that
(a,b)\in S_{x}^{+}
. This is equivalent to
1+\left\lfloor\frac{xb}{1-x}\right\rfloor\leq a\leq 1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor
. Then,
(a+1,b)\in S_{x}^{+}
if and only if
1+\left\lfloor\frac{xb}{1-x}\right\rfloor<a+1\leq 1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor
, so that
1+\left\lfloor\frac{xb}{1-x}\right\rfloor\leq a<1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor
. Moreover,
(a,b+1)\in S_{x}^{+}
if and only if
1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor\leq a\leq 1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor
, so that
a=1+\left\lfloor\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rfloor
.
The fact that for
(a,b)\in S_{x}^{+}
, either
(a+1,b)
or
(a,b+1)
belongs to
S_{x}^{+}
and not both at the same time imply that
(a,b)\mapsto a+b
is a bijection from
S_{x}^{+}
to
\mathbb{Z}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle 0<(1-x)a-xb\leq 1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b<(1-x)(a+b)\leq 1+b

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b=\left\lceil(1-x)(a+b)\right\rceil-1\,,

\displaystyle 0<(1-x)a-xb\leq 1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow a-1\leq x(a+b)<a\,

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow a=\left\lfloor x(a+b)\right\rfloor+1\cdot

(2)

For
a\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle 0\leq(1-x)a-xb<1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\frac{xb}{1-x}\leq a<1+\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\left\lceil\frac{xb}{1-x}\right\rceil\leq a\leq\left\lceil\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}\right\rceil\,,

and (
2.2.3
) holds. The proof of (
2.2.2
) comes from the equivalence

x\leq(1-x)a-xb\leq 1\Longleftrightarrow 0\leq a-\frac{x(b+1)}{1-x}<1

Furthermore,

\displaystyle 0\leq(1-x)a-xb<1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b\leq(1-x)(a+b)<1+b

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow b=\left\lfloor(1-x)(a+b)\right\rfloor\,,

\displaystyle 0\leq(1-x)a-xb<1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow a-1<x(a+b)\leq a\,

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow a=\left\lceil x(a+b)\right\rceil\cdot

The remaining of the reasoning is the same as the case
S_{x}^{+}
.

A.2.
Proof of Lemma
2.7

By definition, there exists a unique bijection
\alpha:S\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
c_{1}\preceq c_{1}
if an only if
f(r)\leq f(s)
. Then,
d_{i}=\mathcal{F}(m,S,c_{i})
if and only if
f(d_{i})=f(c_{i})+m
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
. The first equivalence immediately follows. Moreover, if
f(d_{2})\geq f(d_{1})=f(c_{1})+m
, then
f_{d_{2}}-m\geq f(c_{1})\geq 0
, and we set
c_{2}=f^{-1}\left(f(d_{1})-m\right)
.

A.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.6

By (
4.1.3
), for
i\leq j

\displaystyle\sum_{h=i}^{j}o_{h}^{(k,l)}w_{h}^{(k,l)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=i}^{j}w_{h-1}^{(k,l)}-2w_{h}^{(k,l)}+w_{h+1}^{(k,l)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=i-1}^{j-1}w_{h}^{(k,l)}-2\sum_{h=i}^{j}w_{h}^{(k,l)}+\sum_{h=i+1}^{j+1}w_{h}^{(k,l)}

\displaystyle=w_{i-1}^{(k,l)}-w_{j+1}^{(k,l)}-w_{i}^{(k,l)}+w_{j+1}^{(k,l)}\,\cdot

Therefore, for
1\leq i<j
, as
c_{h}=o_{h}^{(k,l)}+\chi(ih\in\{i,j\})
, (
4.1.5
) follows.

A.4.
Proof of Lemma
4.15

By definition, a forbidden pattern always begins and ends by a positive integer. Therefore, for
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
which that not contain any forbidden pattern,
0\cdot(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
also does not contain any forbidden pattern. Hence, for
n,i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{i}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
if and only if
0\cdot(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{i+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. Moreover, in
{}_{i}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
,
(c_{u})_{u\geq}\prec(d_{u})_{u\geq 1}
if and only if there exists
i\leq j<n
such that
c_{j}<d_{j}
and
c_{u}=d_{u}
for all
j<u<n
. By definition, by setting
(c^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 1}=0\cdot(c_{u})_{u\geq 1}
and
(d^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 1}=0\cdot(d_{u})_{u\geq 1}
, this is equivalent to saying that
c^{\prime}_{j+1}<d_{j^{\prime}+1}
and
c^{\prime}_{u}=d^{\prime}_{u}
for all
n+1>i>j+1
, which is equivalent to
(c^{\prime}_{i})_{i\geq}\prec(d^{\prime}_{i})_{i\geq 1}
in
{}_{i+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
.

To prove the second part of the Lemma, first note that
{}_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n}=\{(0)_{i=1}^{n}\}
and
{}_{n+2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n+1}=\{(0)_{i=1}^{n+1}\}
, so that
0\cdot\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n}=\,_{n+2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n+1}
. For
1\leq j\leq n
, as
0\cdot\,_{j}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}=\,_{j+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
and
\prec
is preserved, we naturally have

0\cdot\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n}\equiv 0\cdot\,_{j}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}=\,_{j+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\equiv\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\,,

so that
0\cdot\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n}=\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{n+1}
with
\prec
preserved.

For
k\geq 4
, we have
0\cdot\,_{i}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}=0\cdot\,_{i}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}=\,_{i+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}=\,_{i+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}
, and
\prec
is preserved. The same holds for
{}_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n}=_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}_{n}
, as

0\cdot\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n}=0\cdot\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}_{n}=\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}_{n+1}=\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n+2}
. To prove the result on
{}_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}
, it suffices to observe that
0\cdot\,^{u}_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}=\,^{u}_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}
for
u\in\{0,1\}
, and that the order
\prec
is preserved on each set.

A.5.
Proof of Lemma
4.16

Let
n
be a positive integer and
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
.

(1)

Suppose that there exists
j\geq n
such that
c_{i}=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+\delta_{i,j}
for
n\leq i\leq j
, and set
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}

0=d_{1}=\cdots=d_{j}\quad,\quad d_{j+1}=c_{j+1}+1\quad\text{and}\quad d_{i}=c_{i}\text{ for }i\geq j+2\,\cdot

We first show that
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. Since
d_{1}=\cdots=d_{n-1}=0
, it suffices to prove that it does not contain neither
o^{(k,l)}_{i}+2
or a forbidden sub-sequence.

(a)

Assume that there is some
i
such that
d_{i}=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+2
. This is only possible when
i=j+1
and
c_{j+1}=o^{(k,l)}_{j+1}+1
. Thus, the sequence
(c_{i})
contains the sub-sequence
(c_{j},c_{j+1})=(o^{(k,l)}_{j}+1,o^{(k,l)}_{j+1}+1)
, which is a forbidden sub-sequence by definition.

(b)

Now assume that there is a forbidden sub-sequence in
(d_{i})
. Since
(d_{i})_{i\geq j+2}=(c_{i})_{i\geq j+2}
, this forbidden sub-sequence necessarily starts from
j+1
, and by (
4.1.1
), there exists
i>j+1
such that
d_{h}=o^{(k,l)}_{h}+\chi(h\in\{j+1,i\})
for
j+1\leq h\leq j
. Therefore,
c_{h}=o^{(k,l)}_{h}+\chi(h\in\{j,i\})
for
j\leq h\leq j
and
(c_{j},\ldots,c_{i})
is a forbidden sub-sequence.

The assumption of the two possible cases of forbidden sub-sequence leads to a contradiction. Thus,
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. In addition,
c_{j+1}<d_{j+1}
and
c_{i}=d_{i}
for
i>j+1
so that
(c_{i})\prec(d_{i})
. We finally prove that
(d_{i})
follows
(c_{i})
in terms of
\prec
in
\,{}_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. Suppose the contrary and assume that there exists
(e_{i})\in\,_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
such that
(c_{i})\prec(e_{i})\prec(d_{i})
.

(a)

Since
c_{j+1}+1=d_{j+1}
and
c_{i}=d_{i}
for
i>j+1
, we necessarily have that
c_{j+1}\leq e_{j+1}\leq c_{j+1}+1=d_{j+1}
and
c_{i}=e_{i}=d_{i}
for
i>j+1
. The fact that
0=d_{i}\leq e_{i}
for
i\leq j
implies that
e_{j+1}<d_{j+1}
, and then
e_{j+1}=c_{j+1}
.

(b)

Note that
c_{j}=o^{(k,l)}_{j}+1\leq e_{j}\leq o^{(k,l)}_{j}+1
and then
e_{j}=o^{(k,l)}_{j}+1=c_{j}
. Also, since
(c_{i})\prec(e_{i})
and
e_{i}=c_{i}
for
i\geq j
and
i<n
, there exists
n\leq g<j
such that
o^{(k,l)}_{g}=c_{g}<e_{g}\leq o^{(k,l)}_{g}+1
and
o^{(k,l)}_{h}=c_{h}=e_{h}
for
g<h<n
. Thus,
e_{h}=o^{(k,l)}_{h}+\chi(h\in\{g,j\})
for
g\leq h\leq j
, and
(e_{g},\ldots,e_{j})
is a forbidden sub-sequence, which contradicts the fact that
(e_{i})
is a
(k,l)
-admissible word.

From what precedes,
(d_{i})
follows
(c_{i})
in terms of
\prec
in
\,{}_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
.

(2)

Suppose that there does not exist
j\geq n
such that
c_{i}=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+\delta_{i,j}
for
n\leq i\leq j
, and set
(d_{i})\in\,_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
such that

d_{n}=c_{n}+1\quad\text{and}\quad d_{i}=c_{i}\text{ for }i\geq n+1
. In the set of integer sequences with the
(n-1)^{th}
first terms equals to
0
, one can check that
(d_{i})
follows
(c_{i})
in terms of
\prec
. It now suffices to show that
(d_{i})\in\,_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. Assume the contrary. As in the previous reasoning, we either have
d_{n}=o^{(k,l)}_{n}+2
or
(d_{i})
contains a forbidden sub-sequence.

(a)

If
d_{n}=o^{(k,l)}_{n}+2
, then
c_{n}=o^{(k,l)}_{n}+1
and we have the fact in
(1)
with
j=n
.

(b)

Suppose now that
(d_{i})
contains a forbidden sub-sequence, and this sub-sequence necessarily starts from
n
and there exist
j>n
such that
d_{i}=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+\chi(h\in\{n,j\})
for
n\leq i\leq j
. Thus,
c_{i}=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+\delta_{h,j}
for
n\leq i\leq j
and we have the fact in
(1)
for
j>n
.

In both cases, the reasoning leads to the fact
(1)
, and we have a contradiction.

A.6.
Proof of Lemma
4.17

For
j=n
,
0=c_{1}=\ldots=c_{n-1}=d_{1}=\cdots=d_{n-1}
, and
d_{n}=c_{n}+1
, so that only
(2)
holds. Now assume that
1\leq j<n
.
Let us write
(c_{1},\cdots,c_{n})\equiv((c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots),c_{n})
. If
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)\neq(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},o_{j}^{(k,l)},\ldots,o_{n-2}^{(k,l)},o_{n-1}^{(k,l)}+1,0,\ldots)
, then the sequence
(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})\equiv((d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots),d_{n})
is such that
c_{n}=d_{n}
and
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots)
follows
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)
in
{}_{j}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. By Lemma
4.16
,
(1)
holds for
1\leq h<n-1
or
(2)
holds. If
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},o_{j}^{(k,l)},\ldots,o_{n-2}^{(k,l)},o_{n-1}^{(k,l)}+1,0,\ldots)
, then
d_{n}=c_{n}+1
, and
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots)=(0,\ldots)
, and
(1)
holds for
h=n-1
.

A.7.
Proof of Lemma
4.20

For
j=n
,
0=c_{1}=\ldots=c_{n-1}=d_{1}=\cdots=d_{n-1}
, and
d_{n}=c_{n}+1
, so that only
(2)
holds. Now assume that
1\leq j<n
.
Let us write
(c_{1},\cdots,c_{n})\equiv((c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots),c_{n})
.

(1)

If
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)\prec(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},o_{j}^{(k,l)},\ldots,o_{n-3}^{(k,l)},o_{n-2}^{(k,l)}+1,0,\ldots)
, then the sequence
(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})\equiv((d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots),d_{n})
is such that
c_{n}=d_{n}
,
0=c_{n-1}=d_{n-1}
, and
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-2},0,\ldots)
follows
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-2},0,\ldots)
in
{}_{j}^{n-1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. By Lemma
4.16
,
(1)
holds for
1\leq h<n-2
or
(2)
holds.

(2)

If
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},o_{j}^{(k,l)},\ldots,o_{n-3}^{(k,l)},o_{n-2}^{(k,l)}+1,0,\ldots)
, then the sequence
(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})\equiv((d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots),d_{n})
is such that
c_{n}=d_{n}
,
1=c_{n-1}+1=d_{n-1}
, and
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-2},0,\ldots)=(0,\ldots)
, and
(1)
holds for
h=n-2
.

(3)

If

(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{n-2\text{ times}},1,0,\ldots)\preceq(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)\prec(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},o_{j}^{(k,l)},\ldots,o_{n-2}^{(k,l)},o_{n-2}^{(k,l)}+2,0,\ldots)\,,

we necessarily have
c_{n-1}\geq 1
, and the sequence
(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})\equiv((d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots),d_{n})
is such that
c_{n}=d_{n}
,
0<c_{n-1}=d_{n-1}
, and
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-2},d_{n-1}-1,0,\ldots)
follows
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-2},c_{n-1},0,\ldots)
in
{}_{j}^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. By Lemma
4.16
,
(1)
holds for
1\leq h\leq n-2
or
(2)
holds.

(4)

If
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},0,\ldots)=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{j-1\text{ times}},o_{j}^{(k,l)},\ldots,o_{n-2}^{(k,l)},o_{n-2}^{(k,l)}+2,0,\ldots)
, then
d_{n}=c_{n}+1
, and
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n-1},0,\ldots)=(0,\ldots)
. Then,
(1)
holds for
h=n-1
.

A.8.
Proof of Lemma
4.24

Let
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.

(1)

As
s^{(k,1)}_{0}+s^{(k,1)}_{2}=s^{(k,1)}_{1}=1
and
s^{(k,1)}_{0}>s^{(k,1)}_{2}
, by (
2.2.1
) with
x=s^{(k,1)}_{0}
and
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, Definition
4.12
and (
4.4.1
) in
(k-2,k-2)
,

\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}\setminus\{((0)_{i\geq 1},(0)_{i\geq 1})\}=\{(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)})\setminus\{(0)_{i\geq 1}\}\times\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}:0\cdot s_{2}\preceq s_{1}\preceq 0\cdot\mathcal{F}(1,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2})\}\,\cdot

Hence

\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)}=\{(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)})\times\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}:0\cdot s_{2}\preceq s_{1}\preceq 0\cdot\mathcal{F}(1,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2})\}\,\cdot

Moreover, by fact
(1.a)
of Lemma
2.5
, if
s
is not special, then
t_{2}=s_{2}
and
t_{1}
is the sequence that follows
s_{1}
in
\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}
. By fact
(1.b)
of Lemma
2.5
, if
s
is special, then
t_{2}=s^{\prime}_{2}
and
t_{1}=s_{1}
.

Finally, if
(t_{1},t_{2})=\mathcal{F}(m,\mathcal{C}^{(1,k)},(s_{1},s_{2}))
, then
(a)
and
(b)
implies that
m=m^{\prime}_{1}+m^{\prime}_{2}
where
t_{i}=\mathcal{F}(m^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{i})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. We then have

0\cdot\mathcal{F}(0,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2})\preceq s_{1}\preceq 0\cdot\mathcal{F}(1,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2})\preceq\cdots\preceq 0\cdot\mathcal{F}(m^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2})\preceq t_{1}\preceq 0\cdot\mathcal{F}(m^{\prime}_{2}+1,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2})\,,

so that

\displaystyle m_{2}

\displaystyle=\sharp\{c\in\,_{2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}:s_{1}\prec c\preceq t_{1}\}

\displaystyle=m^{\prime}_{2}-\chi(s_{1}=0\cdot\mathcal{F}(1,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},s_{2}))+\chi(t_{1}=0\cdot\mathcal{F}(1,\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)},t_{2})\,\cdot

Since
m^{\prime}_{1}=m_{1}+m^{\prime}_{2}
, we obtain (
4.4.8
), and fact
(1)
holds for
d=0
.
Using Lemma
4.15
, we deduce from
d=0
the result for any
d\geq 1
.

(2)

The same goes for fact
(2)
, as
a^{(k,1)}_{n+1}>a^{(k,1)}_{n-1}
, and
a^{(k,1)}_{n+1}+a^{(k,1)}_{n-1}=a^{(1,k)}_{n}
. Using (
2.2.3
) with
x=a^{(k,1)}_{n+1}/a^{(1,k)}_{n}
and
b\geq 0
, Definition
4.12
and (
4.4.6
), fact
(2)
is satisfied for
d=0
. Using Lemma
4.15
, we deduce from
d=0
the result for any
d\geq 1
.

A.9.
Proof of Lemma
8.3

For
t-2\geq j\geq 1

\nu_{2j}-1-\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j+1}\right\rfloor=\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2j}\right\rceil-1-\lambda_{2j+1}\,,

for
t-1\geq j\geq 1
,

\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j-1}\right\rceil-1-\nu_{2j}=\lambda_{2j+1}-1-\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2j+2}\right\rfloor\,,

and
\lambda_{1}-1-\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\lambda_{2}\right\rfloor=m
.
Using Remark
2.4
,
\lambda\in\mathcal{L}^{(k,l)}
if and only if
(\nu,m)\in\mathcal{L}^{(l,k)}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Finally, we have that

|\lambda|_{e}=\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\lambda_{2j}=\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\nu_{2j-1}=|\nu|_{o}

and

\displaystyle|\lambda|_{o}

\displaystyle=m+1+\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\nu_{1}\right\rfloor+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t-2}\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j-1}\right\rceil+\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j+1}\right\rfloor-\nu_{2j}\right)+\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2t-3}\right\rceil-1-\nu_{2t-2}

\displaystyle=m-\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\nu_{2t-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\left\lceil s_{2}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j-1}\right\rceil+\left\lfloor s_{0}^{(k,l)}\nu_{2j-1}\right\rfloor

\displaystyle=m-\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\nu_{2t-2}+l\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\nu_{2j-1}

\displaystyle=m+l\cdot|\nu|_{o}-|\nu|_{e}\,\cdot

Appendix B
Proof of Propositions

B.1.
Proof of Proposition
2.2

(1)

By (
2.1.1
), for
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}a^{(k,l)}_{2m}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n}a^{(l,k)}_{2m-1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}\cdot\frac{(u^{2n-1}-u^{-2n+1})(u^{2m}-u^{-2m})-(u^{2n}-u^{-2n})(u^{2m-1}-u^{-2m+1})}{(u-u^{-1})^{2}}\,

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}\cdot\frac{-u^{2n-2m-1}-u^{-2n+2m+1}+u^{2n-2m+1}+u^{-2n+2m-1}}{(u-u^{-1})^{2}}\,

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}\cdot\frac{(u^{2n-2m}-u^{-2n+2m})(u-u^{-1})}{(u-u^{-1})^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=a^{(k,l)}_{2n-2m}\,,

and

\displaystyle a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}a^{(k,l)}_{2m+1}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n}a^{(l,k)}_{2m}

\displaystyle=\frac{(u^{2n-1}-u^{-2n+1})(u^{2m+1}-u^{-2m-1})-(u^{2n}-u^{-2n})(u^{2m}-u^{-2m})}{(u-u^{-1})^{2}}\,

\displaystyle=\frac{-u^{2n-2m-2}-u^{-2n+2m+2}+u^{2n-2m}+u^{-2n+2m}}{(u-u^{-1})^{2}}\,

\displaystyle=\frac{(u^{2n-2m-1}-u^{-2n+2m+1})(u-u^{-1})}{(u-u^{-1})^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=a^{(k,l)}_{2n-2m-1}\,\cdot

and (
2.1.4
) follows. Analogously, we prove (
2.1.5
).

(2)

By (
2.1.1
), for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle a^{(k,l)}_{2n}-\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}u\cdot a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}\cdot\frac{(u^{2n}-u^{-2n})-u(u^{2n-1}-u^{-2n+1})}{u-u^{-1}}\,

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}\cdot\frac{u^{-2n+2}-u^{-2n}}{u-u^{-1}}\,

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}u^{-2n+1}

and

\displaystyle a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}-\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}u\cdot a^{(l,k)}_{2n}

\displaystyle=\frac{(u^{2n+1}-u^{-2n-1})-u(u^{2n}-u^{-2n})}{u-u^{-1}}\,

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{-2n+1}-u^{-2n-1}}{u-u^{-1}}\,

\displaystyle=u^{-2n}\,\cdot

Since
(a^{(l,k)}_{2n})_{n\geq 1}
and
(a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1})_{n\geq 1}
are both increasing sequence of positive integers, they tend to
\infty
, so that, with the fact that
u^{-1}\leq 1
, both sequences
(a^{(k,l)}_{2n}/a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1})_{n\geq 1}
and
(a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}/a^{(l,k)}_{2n})_{n\geq 1}
tend to
\sqrt{\frac{l}{k}}u
. Moreover, by (
2.1.4
) and (
2.1.5
), for
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}}-\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}=\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}

\displaystyle\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}-\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+2}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n+1}}=\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}a^{(l,k)}_{2n+1}}

so that
(a^{(k,l)}_{n}/a^{(l,k)}_{n-1})_{n\geq 2}
is decreasing.

B.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.7

(1)

To prove (
4.1.6
), it suffices to prove that

(c_{i})\prec(d_{i})\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,l)}((c_{i}))<\Gamma_{(k,l)}((d_{i}))\,\cdot

In fact,
\prec
is a total strict order, i.e for
(c_{i})\neq(d_{i})
, we either have
(c_{i})\prec(d_{i})
or
(c_{i})\succ(d_{i})
, and the previous implication yields

(c_{i})\succ(d_{i})\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,l)}((c_{i}))>\Gamma_{(k,l)}((d_{i}))\,\cdot

(a)

Let
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
and
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}
such that
(c_{i})\prec(d_{i})
, and let
n
be the unique positive integer such that
c_{n}<d_{n}
and
c_{i}=d_{i}
for
i>n
. Thus,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,l)}((d_{i}))-\Gamma_{(k,l)}((c_{i}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(d_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\geq w^{(k,l)}_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}\,\cdot

By (
4.1.4
), we have that

w^{(k,l)}_{n}+w^{(k,l)}_{0}-w^{(k,l)}_{1}-w^{(k,l)}_{n-1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}o^{(k,l)}_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}\,\cdot

Therefore, if
c_{i}\leq o^{(k,l)}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
, then

\displaystyle w^{(k,l)}_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle=(w^{(k,l)}_{1}-w^{(k,l)}_{0})+w^{(k,l)}_{n-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle\geq(w^{(k,l)}_{1}-w^{(k,l)}_{0})+w^{(k,l)}_{n-1}

\displaystyle>0\,\cdot

Now suppose that there exists
t\geq 1
indices
i
, namely
1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{t}\leq n-1
, such that
c_{i}>o^{(k,l)}_{i}
. They then satisfy
c_{i}=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+1
.
As
c_{i_{j}},\cdots,c_{i_{j+1}}
is not a forbidden sub-sequence, we necessarily have that the set
\{i_{j}+1,i_{j+1}-1\}
is not empty and contains a integer
i^{\prime}_{j}
such that
c_{i^{\prime}_{j}}<o^{(k,l)}_{i^{\prime}_{j}}
. Then,

\displaystyle w^{(k,l)}_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle=w^{(k,l)}_{1}-w^{(k,l)}_{0}+(w^{(k,l)}_{n-1}-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{t}})+\left(\sum_{i=i_{t}+1}^{n-1}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{i_{1}-1}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\left(-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{j}}+\sum_{i=i_{j}+1}^{i_{j+1}-1}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(w^{(k,l)}_{1}-w^{(k,l)}_{0})+(w^{(k,l)}_{n-1}-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{t}})+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}w^{(k,l)}_{i^{\prime}_{j}}-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{j}}

\displaystyle\geq(w^{(k,l)}_{1}-w^{(k,l)}_{0})+t-1

\displaystyle>0\,\cdot

and we conclude. The fact that
\prec
is a total strict order on
\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
implies that
\Gamma_{(k,l)}
is indeed injective.

(b)

We now prove that, for
(s_{i}^{(k,l)})=(a^{(k,l)}_{i})
,
\Gamma_{(k,l)}
is surjective on
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Consider the function described as the inverse. For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, since
(a^{(k,l)}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is increasing, tends to
\infty
and
a^{(k,l)}_{0}=0
,
n
is simply equal to
\min\{i:a^{(k,l)}_{i}>m\}
. Moreover, the recursive construction of
c_{i}
for
1\leq i<n
implies that

\displaystyle m-\sum_{j=i}^{n-1}c_{j}a^{(k,l)}_{j}

\displaystyle=m-\sum_{j=i+1}^{n-1}c_{j}a^{(k,l)}_{j}-a^{(k,l)}_{i}\left\lfloor\frac{m-\sum_{j=i+1}^{n-1}c_{j}a^{(k,l)}_{j}}{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}\right\rfloor

\displaystyle\in\{0,\ldots,a^{(k,l)}_{i}-1\}\,\cdot

Thus, the fact that
a^{(k,l)}_{1}=1
yields
m=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}c_{j}a^{(k,l)}_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}c_{j}a^{(k,l)}_{j}
. Furthermore, we recursively have that

\displaystyle 0\leq c_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-1}{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}\right\rfloor

\displaystyle=\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}+a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}-(a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}+1)}{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}\right\rfloor

\displaystyle=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+2+\left\lfloor\frac{-(a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}+1)}{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}\right\rfloor

\displaystyle=o^{(k,l)}_{i}+1

since, by the fact that
(a^{(k,l)}_{j})_{j\geq 0}
is a increasing sequence of non-negative integers,
-1\leq\frac{-(a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}+1)}{a^{(k,l)}_{i}}<0
. Then
c_{i}\in\{0,\ldots,o^{(k,l)}_{i}+1\}
, so that the sequence
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
satisfied the two first fact for the definition of
(k,l)
-sequences. Finally, if we suppose by that a forbidden sub-sequence
c_{i},\ldots,c_{j}
occurs in
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1})
, by (
4.1.4
),

\displaystyle a^{(k,l)}_{j+1}

\displaystyle>m-\sum_{h=j+1}^{n-1}c_{h}a^{(k,l)}_{h}=\sum_{h=1}^{j}c_{h}a^{(k,l)}_{h}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{h=i}^{j}c_{h}a^{(k,l)}_{h}=a^{(k,l)}_{j+1}+a^{(k,l)}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\geq a^{(k,l)}_{j+1}\,,

and we have a contradiction. Hence,
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}_{(k,l)}
, and
\Gamma_{(k,l)}
is surjective as
m=\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i}
. Thus,
\Gamma_{(k,l)}
is a bijection. We finally conclude by observing that, by (
4.1.6
),

\left[\{0,\ldots,a^{(k,l)}_{n}-1\}\right]^{(k,l)}=\{(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}\prec(\delta_{i,n})_{i\geq 1}\}=\,^{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}

(c)

As
{}_{n}\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}\preceq\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}
and the relation
\preceq
is transitive, to prove (
4.1.2
), it suffices to show that for
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
such that
(c_{i})\succ\,_{n}\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}
,
(c_{i})\succ\,_{n}\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}
. Set
\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}=(m_{i})_{i\geq 1}
and assume that
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
and
(c_{i})\succ\,_{n}\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}
. As both sequences have their
(n-1)^{th}
first parts equal to
0
, this means that there exists
i\geq n
such
c_{i}>m_{i}
and
c_{j}=m_{j}
for
j>i
, and this implies that
(c_{i})\succ\left[m\right]^{(k,l)}
.

(2)

Let
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
and
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}
such that
(c_{i})\ll(d_{i})
, and let
n
be the unique positive integer such that
c_{n}<d_{n}
and
c_{i}=d_{i}
for
1\leq i<n
. Thus, for an
m>n
such that
c_{i}=0
for
i>m

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,l)}((d_{i}))-\Gamma_{(k,l)}((c_{i}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\geq w^{(k,l)}_{n}-\sum_{i=n+1}^{m}c_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}\,\cdot

By (
4.1.4
), we have that

w^{(k,l)}_{n}+w^{(k,l)}_{m+1}-w^{(k,l)}_{n+1}-w^{(k,l)}_{m}=\sum_{i=n+1}^{m}o^{(k,l)}_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}\,\cdot

Therefore, if
c_{i}\leq o^{(k,l)}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
, then

\displaystyle w^{(k,l)}_{n}-\sum_{i=n+1}^{m}c_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle=(w^{(k,l)}_{m}-w^{(k,l)}_{m+1})+w^{(k,l)}_{n+1}+\sum_{i=n+1}^{m}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle\geq(w^{(k,l)}_{m}-w^{(k,l)}_{m+1})+w^{(k,l)}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\geq 0\,\cdot

Now suppose that there exists
t\geq 1
indices
i
, namely
n+1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{t}\leq m
, such that
c_{i}>o^{(k,l)}_{i}
. As in the previous case, we necessarily have that the set
\{i_{j^{\prime}-1}+1,i_{j}-1\}
is not empty and contains a integer
i^{\prime}_{j}
such that
c_{i^{\prime}_{j}}<o^{(k,l)}_{i^{\prime}_{j}}
. Then,

\displaystyle w^{(k,l)}_{n}-\sum_{i=n+1}^{m}c_{i}w^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle=(w^{(k,l)}_{m}-w^{(k,l)}_{m+1})+(w^{(k,l)}_{n+1}-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{1}})+\left(\sum_{i=n+1}^{i_{1}-1}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sum_{i=i_{t}+1}^{m}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\right)+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\left(-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{j}}+\sum_{i=i_{j-1}+1}^{i_{j}-1}(o^{(k,l)}_{i}-c_{i})w^{(k,l)}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(w^{(k,l)}_{m}-w^{(k,l)}_{m+1})+(w^{(k,l)}_{n+1}-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{1}})+\sum_{j=2}^{t}w^{(k,l)}_{i^{\prime}_{j}}-w^{(k,l)}_{i_{j}}

\displaystyle\geq(w^{(k,l)}_{m}-w^{(k,l)}_{m+1})

\displaystyle\geq 0\,\cdot

and this yields (
4.1.7
).
We also observe that,
by (
4.1.6
), for
(c_{i})\in\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
,
(c_{i})\ll(\delta_{i,n})
so that
\Gamma_{(k,l)}(\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)})\subset(0,s_{n}^{(k,l)})
.

B.3.
Proof of Proposition
4.19

(1)

(a)

For
n\geq 1
, and
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}
that follows
(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
in
{}_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}
, we equivalently have that
0\cdot(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}
follows
0\cdot(c_{i})_{i\geq 1}
in
{}_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. Hence

0\cdot(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}
belongs to

{}_{n+2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
if and only if the first fact of Lemma
4.16
occurs, and by using the corresponding notations, (
4.3.1
) and (
4.1.4
) give

\displaystyle\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}

\displaystyle=a^{(k,l)}_{j+2}-a^{(k,l)}_{j+1}\sum_{i=n}^{j}o_{i+1}^{(k,l)}\cdot a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}

\displaystyle=a^{(k,l)}_{n+1}-a^{(k,l)}_{n}\,\cdot

On the other hand,
0\cdot(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}
does not belong to

{}_{n+2}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
if and only if the second fact of Lemma
4.16
occurs, and (
4.3.2
)
gives
\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=a^{(k,l)}_{n+1}
.
As
a^{(k,l)}_{n}\neq 0
, by Lemma
4.15
, we have the equivalences

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=a^{(k,l)}_{n+1}-a^{(k,l)}_{n}

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\notin\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=a^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\,\cdot

(b)

Let us now set the function

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\ (c_{i})_{i\geq 1}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}(a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-s^{(k,l)}_{0}a^{(l,k)}_{i})\,\cdot

By (
2.1.6
) and Proposition
4.7
, this is exactly
\Gamma_{(l,k)}
for
w_{i}^{(l,k)}=s^{(k,l)}_{i+1}
. In particular,
(w_{i}^{(l,k)})_{n\geq 1}
is non-increasing, and fact
(2)
of Proposition
4.7
implies that

\Gamma_{(l,k)}(_{n}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)})\subset(0;w_{n-1}^{(l,k)})=(0;s^{(k,l)}_{n})
.

Hence,
\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1}),\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})\in(0;s^{(k,l)}_{n})
and
\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1})\neq 0
(as
(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\neq 0
and
s^{(k,l)}_{i}>0
).
In addition,

\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1})-\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})(a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-s^{(k,l)}_{0}a^{(l,k)}_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(d_{i}-c_{i})s^{(k,l)}_{i+1}\,\cdot

By Lemma
4.16
and (
4.1.4
),

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\in\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1})-\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})=s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}-s^{(k,l)}_{n}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})=s^{(k,l)}_{n}-s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}+\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1})\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})>s^{(k,l)}_{n}-s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\,,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\notin\,_{n+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1})-\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})=s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})=\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i\geq 1})-s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\leq s^{(k,l)}_{n}-s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i\geq 1})\leq s^{(k,l)}_{n}-s^{(k,l)}_{n+1}\,,

and (
1
) and (
1
) follow.

(2)

(a)

Let
n\geq 1
,
2n-1\geq j\geq 1
, and assume that
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}
follows
(c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}
in
{}_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}
. Analogously to the previous reasoning, by Lemma
4.17
and (
4.1.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\in\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i=j}^{2n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=a^{(k,l)}_{j+1}-a^{(k,l)}_{j}

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i=j}^{2n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=a^{(k,l)}_{j+1}\,\cdot

(b)

Set the function

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}\colon\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\ (c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{2n-1}c_{i}(a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n}a^{(l,k)}_{i})\,\cdot

By (
2.1.4
), for

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-i}=\sum_{i=0}^{2n-2}c_{2n-1-i}a^{(k,l)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2n-2}c_{2n-1-i}a^{(k,l)}_{i}\,\cdot

Note that, as
i
and
2n-1-i
have different parities,
o^{(l,k)}_{2n-1-i}=o_{i}^{(k,l)}
, so that if
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{2n-2},0,\ldots)\in\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}
, then
(c_{2n-2},\ldots,c_{1},0,\cdots)\in\,^{2n-j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
. Therefore, by fact
(1)
of Proposition
4.7
,

\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}),\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})\in\{0,a^{(k,l)}_{2n-j}-1\}\,\cdot

Hence, by (
4.1.4
) and Lemma
4.17
,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\in\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})-\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})=a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-j}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n-j}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})=a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-j}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n-j}+\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})\geq a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-j}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n-j}\,,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i\geq 1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})-\Gamma_{(l,k)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})=a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-j}\,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})=\Gamma_{(l,k)}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})-a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-j}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(l,k)}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1})<a^{(k,l)}_{2n-j}-a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1-j}\,,

and (
2
) and (
2
) follow.

(3)

The reasoning is the same, by using (
2.1.5
) instead of (
2.1.4
), the function

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,l)}^{2n}\colon\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}_{2n}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\ (c_{i})_{i=1}^{2n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{2n}c_{i}(a^{(k,l)}_{2n}a^{(l,k)}_{i+1}-a^{(l,k)}_{2n+1}a^{(l,k)}_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{2n}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{2n-i}\,,

and observing that

(c_{1},\ldots,c_{2n-1},0,\ldots)\in\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
implies
(c_{2n-1},\ldots,c_{1},0,\cdots)\in\,^{2n+1-j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,l)}
.

B.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.21

The case
n
even only consists of facts
(2)
and
(3)
for
(k-2,k-2)
instead of
(k,l)
. We now consider the case
n
odd, written
2n-1
.

(1)

For positive integer
n
,
\lfloor n-1\rfloor\geq j\geq 1
, and
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
follows
(c_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
in
{}_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}
, we have by fact
(2)
of Lemma
4.20
that

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow(d_{n}-c_{n})a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}=a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}

Now suppose that fact
(1)
occurs in Lemma
4.20
, which is equivalent to saying that
(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}
.
In the case
h=n-1
, we have by (
4.3.11
) that

c_{i}=o_{i}^{(k-2)}+2\delta_{i,n-1}
and
d_{i}=0
for
j\leq i\leq n-1
, and
d_{n}=c_{n}+1
. Therefore, by (
4.1.4
),

\displaystyle(d_{n}-c_{n})a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}

\displaystyle=a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}-2a^{(k,1)}_{2n}-\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}o_{i}^{(k-2)}a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}

\displaystyle=a_{n+1}^{(k-2)}-a_{n}^{(k-2)}-\sum_{i=j+1}^{n}o_{i}^{(k-2)}a_{i}^{(k-2)}

\displaystyle=a_{j+1}^{(k-2)}-a_{j}^{(k-2)}

\displaystyle=a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}-a^{(k,1)}_{2j}\,\cdot

In the remaining case
j\leq h\leq n-2
, by (
4.3.11
) and (
4.1.4
)

\displaystyle(d_{n}-c_{n})a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}

\displaystyle=a^{(k,1)}_{2h+4}-a^{(k,1)}_{2h+2}-\sum_{i=j}^{h}o_{i}^{(k-2)}a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}

\displaystyle=a_{h+2}^{(k-2)}-a_{h+1}^{(k-2)}-\sum_{i=j+1}^{h+1}o_{i}^{(k-2)}a_{i}^{(k-2)}

\displaystyle=a_{j+1}^{(k-2)}-a_{j}^{(k-2)}

\displaystyle=a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}-a^{(k,1)}_{2j}\,\cdot

Thus,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\in\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow(d_{n}-c_{n})a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}=a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}-a^{(k,1)}_{2j}\,,

and since
a^{(k,1)}_{2j}\neq 0
,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\in\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow(d_{n}-c_{n})a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}=a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}-a^{(k,1)}_{2j}\,,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow(d_{n}-c_{n})a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}(d_{i}-c_{i})a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}=a^{(k,1)}_{2j+2}\,\cdot

(2)

Let us now set the function

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}\colon\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\ (c_{i})_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}(a^{(1,k)}_{2n-1}a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}-a^{(1,k)}_{2i+1}a^{(l,k)}_{2i})\,\cdot

We then have by (
2.1.4
) and (
2.1.5
) that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}(a_{n}^{(k-2)}\cdot a_{i+1}^{(k-2)}-a_{n+1}^{(k-2)}\cdot a_{i}^{(k-2)})+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}(a_{n-1}^{(k-2)}\cdot a_{i+1}^{(k-2)}-a_{n}^{(k-2)}\cdot a_{i}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}a_{n-i}^{(k-2)}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}a_{n-1-i}^{(k-2)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{n-i}a_{i}^{(k-2)}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}c_{n-1-i}a_{i}^{(k-2)}

Observe that
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-2},0,\ldots)\in\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}
implies
(c_{n-2},\ldots,c_{1},0,\ldots)\in\,^{n-j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}
, and by fact
(1)
of Proposition
4.7
,

0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}c_{n-1-i}a_{i}^{(k-2)}<a_{n-j}^{(k-2)}\,\cdot

On one hand, if
c_{n-1}=0
, then
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-2},0,\ldots)\in\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}
implies
(0,c_{n-2},\ldots,c_{1},0,\ldots)\in\,^{n+1-j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}
, so that

0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{n-i}a_{i}^{(k-2)}<a_{n+1-j}^{(k-2)}\,\cdot

One the other hand, if
c_{n-1}>0
, then
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-2},c_{n-1}-1,0,\ldots)\in\,_{j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}
implies that

(c_{n-1}-1,c_{n-2},\ldots,c_{1},0,\ldots)\in\,^{n+1-j}\mathcal{C}^{(k-2)}
, so that

0<\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{n-i}a_{i}^{(k-2)}=a_{1}^{(k-2)}+(c_{n-1}-1)a_{1}^{(k-2)}+\sum_{i=2}^{n-1}c_{n-i}a_{i}^{(k-2)}\leq a_{n+1-j}^{(k-2)}\,\cdot

Therefore,
\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})\in(0;a_{n+1-j}^{(k-2)}+a_{n+1-j}^{(k-2)}(=(0;a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}(
.
Moreover,

\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})-\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(d_{n-i}-c_{n-i})(a_{i}^{(k-2)}+a_{i-1}^{(k-2)})

Hence,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})-\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})=(a_{n-j}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-j-1}^{(k-2)})\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})=\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})-a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})<a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}-a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}\,,

We now study the cases of occurrence of fact
(1)
in Lemma
4.20
. When
h=n-1
, by (
4.1.4
),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})-\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})

\displaystyle=-2(a_{1}^{(k-2)}+a_{0}^{(k-2)})-\sum_{i=1}^{n-j}o_{n-i}^{(k-2)}(a_{i}^{(k-2)}+a_{i-1}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle=-(a_{0}^{(k-2)}+a_{-1}^{(k-2)})-(a_{1}^{(k-2)}+a_{0}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle\qquad-(a_{n+1-j}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-j}^{(k-2)})+(a_{n-j}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-j-1}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle=a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}-a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}\,\cdot

When
h\leq n-2
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})-\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})

\displaystyle=(a_{n-1-h}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-2-h}^{(k-2)})-(a_{n-h}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-1-h}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{i=n-h}^{n-j}o_{n-i}^{(k-2)}(a_{i}^{(k-2)}+a_{i-1}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle=-(a_{n+1-j}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-j}^{(k-2)})+(a_{n-j}^{(k-2)}+a_{n-j-1}^{(k-2)})

\displaystyle=a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}-a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}\,\cdot

Therefore,

\displaystyle(d_{i})_{i=1}^{2n-1}\notin\,_{j+1}\mathcal{C}^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})-\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})=a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}-a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})=\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((d_{i})_{i=1}^{n})+a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}-a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}\,,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Gamma_{(k,1)}^{2n-1}((c_{i})_{i=1}^{n})\geq a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1-2j}-a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1-2j}\,\cdot

and (
4.21
) and (
4.21
) follow.

B.5.
Proof of Proposition
4.22

(1)

Applying fact
(1)
of Proposition
4.19
for
n=1
,

\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}(a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-s^{(k,l)}_{0}a^{(l,k)}_{i})=\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}s^{(k,l)}_{i+1}\in(0;s^{(k,l)}_{1})=(0;1)\,

Moreover, there exists
i\geq 1
such that
c_{i}\neq 0
. Thus,

0<\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-s^{(k,l)}_{0}\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(l,k)}_{i}\leq 1\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=\left\lfloor s^{(k,l)}_{0}\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(l,k)}_{i}\right\rfloor+1\,\cdot

Therefore, for
(r,s)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, by Remark
2.4
,

\displaystyle r>s^{(k,l)}_{0}s\Longleftrightarrow r\geq\lfloor s^{(k,l)}_{0}s\rfloor+1

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq\left[\lfloor s^{(k,l)}_{0}s\rfloor+1\right]^{(k,l)}=0\cdot\left[s\right]^{(l,k)}\,,

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\,_{2}[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq 0\cdot\left[s\right]^{(l,k)}\,\cdot

For
r>s^{(k,l)}_{0}
, this implies that

s^{(k,l)}_{0}\lceil r/s^{(k,l)}_{0}\rceil\geq r>s^{(k,l)}_{0}(\lceil r/s^{(k,l)}_{0}\rceil-1)\Longleftrightarrow 0\cdot\left[s^{(k,l)}_{0}\lceil r/s^{(k,l)}_{0}\rceil\right]^{(l,k)}\succ\,_{2}[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq\left[s^{(k,l)}_{0}(\lceil r/s^{(k,l)}_{0}\rceil-1)\right]^{(l,k)}\,,

so that
{}_{2}[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq\left[s^{(k,l)}_{0}(\lceil r/s^{(k,l)}_{0}\rceil-1)\right]^{(l,k)}
, and (
4.4.1
) follows.
Note that
r\geq 0=s=s^{(k,l)}_{0}s
, we still have
{}_{2}[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq 0\cdot\left[s\right]^{(l,k)}
. As by definition,

{}_{2}[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq 0\cdot\left[s\right]^{(l,k)}\Longleftrightarrow[r]^{(k,l)}\succeq 0\cdot\left[s\right]^{(l,k)}\,,

(
4.4.2
) is immediate.

(2)

By fact
(2)
of Proposition
4.19
for
j=1
,

0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}}\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(l,k)}_{i}<\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n-1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}}=1\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{2n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n-1}}\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(l,k)}_{i}\right\rceil\,\cdot

As the previous reasoning, this leads to (
4.4.3
) for
n
odd. For the case
n
even, it suffices to consider in fact
(3)
of Proposition
4.19
,
j=1
and
(l,k)
instead of
(k,l)
, and we obtain

0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2n}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}-\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(l,k)}_{i}<\frac{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}=1\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{2n}c_{i}a^{(k,l)}_{i+1}=\left\lceil\frac{a^{(k,l)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(l,k)}_{2n}}\sum_{i\geq 1}c_{i}a^{(l,k)}_{i}\right\rceil\,\cdot

From (
4.4.3
) and the fact that
a^{(k,l)}_{2j-1}=a^{(l,k)}_{2j-1}
, (
4.4.4
) and (
4.4.5
) are immediate.

n

instead of

\displaystyle c_{n}a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}-\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}}\left(c_{n}a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,1)}_{2i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,1)}_{2i+2}-\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{2n+1}}{a^{(k,1)}_{2n-1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}c_{i}a^{(k,1)}_{2i}\right)\in(0,1(\,\cdot

B.7.

(1)

For

r>s^{(k,1)}_{0}s\Longleftrightarrow r\geq\lfloor s^{(k,1)}_{0}s\rfloor+1

and (

. Note that

.

. Hence, (

,

and (

) follows from definition

.

r\geq\frac{a^{(1,k)}_{n+1}}{a^{(k,1)}_{n}}s\Longleftrightarrow\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{n}}{a^{(k,1)}_{n+1}}r\geq s\Longleftrightarrow\left\lfloor\frac{a^{(k,1)}_{n}}{a^{(1,k)}_{n+1}}s\right\rfloor

and (
4.4.17
) follows from definition

.
Hence, (

) and (

) follows from (

) for the odd cases and (

) for the even cases, and (

) and (

) follows from (

) for the even cases and (
